## Batch — split the builtin: reviewer command in release-flow delegateReview (no ENOENT) (Unreleased)

> Found while cutting v0.1.94: a `release-flow --cut` with a builtin reviewer never ran the reviewer. When `CW_AGENT_COMMAND=builtin:claude` (or any `builtin:<vendor>`), `resolveAgentConfig` expands it to a SINGLE unsplit string `cfg.command = "node /abs/claude-p-agent.js {{input}} {{result}}"` with `cfg.args` undefined. `delegateReview` then called `spawnSync(cfg.command, [], { shell: false })`, which tried to exec a binary literally named `"node /abs/... {{input}} {{result}}"` → instant ENOENT, which the code mislabeled as "reviewer agent exited (timeout/no-exit)", and the `{{input}}/{{result}}` placeholders never got substituted. A plain `CW_AGENT_COMMAND="claude -p {{input}}"` worked because `resolveAgentConfig` splits env/flag commands — but a builtin-expanded (or durable-file) command is one unsplit string that bypasses that split. The orchestrator already handles this (src/execution-backend/agent.ts `resolveAgentInvocation` splits a whitespace command into binary + argv); `delegateReview` did not. Release-tooling only — no user-facing surface.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Make `delegateReview` (scripts/release-flow.js) split `cfg.command` on whitespace into `bin` + inline args the SAME way the orchestrator does, so a builtin:/durable-file command spawns the real binary instead of ENOENT-ing on an unsplit string. Build the argv as `[...inlineArgs, ...(cfg.args||[])].map(a => substitute(a, subMap))` (explicit `cfg.args` still follow the inline args), `spawnSync(bin, args, { shell:false })`, and update the `[2/3] reviewer` log + the spawn to use `bin`/`args`. Also surface a real spawn error: a new `if (r.error) die(...)` reports the ENOENT/ETIMEDOUT message instead of the old catch-all "(timeout/no-exit)" that hid this for releases. A plain `CW_AGENT_COMMAND="claude -p {{input}}"` is already pre-split by `resolveAgentConfig`, so it is unchanged (no inline args). RED LINE kept: argv-style, `shell:false`, no model SDK, no API key. | plugins/cool-workflow/scripts/release-flow.js (split bin+args; r.error surfaced) + plugins/cool-workflow/test/release-flow-smoke.js (new Case 1b + `fileCommand` seam in runFlow) + ITERATION_LOG.md | New release-flow-smoke Case 1b drives a builtin-shape command via a durable `agent-config.json` (one multi-token `command` string, no `args` array — the exact shape `builtin:` expansion leaves), asserts exit 0, an APPROVED verdict file written, and the `[2/3] reviewer — delegating to: node ` log proving the binary split out. MUTATION proof: reverting the spawn back to `spawnSync(cfg.command, [])` turns Case 1b RED (actual 1, expected 0); the fix turns it GREEN. Regressions: all other release-flow-smoke cases still green. | BUILD n/a (no .ts changed; dist/agent-config.js already current); release-flow-smoke OK (+ mutation RED→GREEN); onramp:check OK; parity:check OK; index:check OK | no (dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

## Batch — make all four vendors run live + a pre-release vendor preflight (Unreleased)

> CW says claude, codex, gemini, and deepseek all work, but only claude ran. Three adapters were broken against the INSTALLED CLIs, and the smokes used fake PATH shims that took any args and any output shape — so the drift shipped unseen. This batch fixes each adapter against the real CLI, routes deepseek + gemini through opencode (where the user's keys live), gives codex back its model name for provenance, and adds `scripts/vendor-preflight.js` — a LIVE all-vendor check wired into `release-flow --cut` that HARD-BLOCKS the cut when any promised vendor is not live. Every fix is proven with a real API call, not only a shim.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Fix the broken vendor adapters against the installed CLIs. codex 0.139 dropped `exec --ask-for-approval` (passing it made codex exit 2, so the run parked) — drop the flag. opencode 1.17.11 `run` takes the message as a POSITIONAL arg (there is no `--prompt`) and its `--format json` now emits `{type,part}` JSONL (assistant text at `part.text` grouped by `part.messageID`, usage at `step_finish` `part.tokens`, no model field) — pass the prompt positionally, add an optional `--model` from `CW_OPENCODE_MODEL`/`CW_OPENCODE_LABEL`, and rewrite the parser to read the new shape (older shapes kept as a fallback). codex carries no model in its JSONL, so read the configured model from `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml` for provenance. | plugins/cool-workflow/scripts/agents/{codex-agent.js, opencode-agent.js} + plugins/cool-workflow/test/{codex-agent-wrapper-smoke.js, opencode-agent-wrapper-smoke.js} (shims re-cut to the REAL CLI output; codex model now from a throwaway `CODEX_HOME/config.toml`) + ITERATION_LOG.md | codex-agent-wrapper-smoke OK; opencode-agent-wrapper-smoke OK. LIVE: codex exit 0 (`model: gpt-5.5`, real usage, `cw:result` written); deepseek+gemini via opencode exit 0 (below). The old shims accepted the bad flag / fake events — that is why this was invisible. | BUILD OK (no .ts change); listed smokes OK | no (dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |
| 2 | Route deepseek + gemini through opencode, where the user's keys live (`opencode auth.json` has `deepseek:api` + `google:api`; the standalone `gemini` CLI has no key). Both are thin variants of the ONE opencode runner: a 3-line shim sets `CW_OPENCODE_LABEL` + the model, then `require`s opencode-agent.js. `builtin:deepseek` → deepseek-agent.js (`deepseek/deepseek-chat`, override `CW_DEEPSEEK_MODEL`). `builtin:gemini` → gemini-opencode-agent.js (`google/gemini-3.5-flash`, override `CW_GEMINI_MODEL`); the native Gemini CLI wrapper is kept as `builtin:gemini-cli`. | plugins/cool-workflow/scripts/agents/{deepseek-agent.js (new), gemini-opencode-agent.js (new), builtin-templates.json (gemini→opencode, +gemini-cli, deepseek→shim)} + plugins/cool-workflow/test/{deepseek-agent-wrapper-smoke.js (new), gemini-opencode-agent-wrapper-smoke.js (new), gemini-agent-wrapper-smoke.js (alias → gemini-cli)} + ITERATION_LOG.md | deepseek + gemini-opencode wrapper smokes OK (model selection, model override, deepseek/gemini live trace label, builtin alias); native gemini smoke OK. LIVE: deepseek exit 0 (`deepseek/deepseek-chat`); gemini exit 0 (`google/gemini-3.5-flash`) — both wrote a real `cw:result`. | BUILD OK; listed smokes OK; agent-delegation-drive-smoke OK | no (dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |
| 3 | Add the pre-release vendor preflight the operator asked for (policy: LIVE calls, HARD-BLOCK). `scripts/vendor-preflight.js` runs EACH builtin wrapper against a throwaway git repo with a trivial question and checks it returns a real result; any vendor that is missing/unauthed/empty is a FAIL and the gate exits 1 (no "skip"). Wired into `release-flow.js` as step `[1b/3]` AFTER the deterministic gate: runs on `--cut` only (live keys live on the release machine), skipped on `--check`; escape hatch `CW_SKIP_VENDOR_PREFLIGHT=1`; test seam `CW_RELEASE_FLOW_PREFLIGHT_CMD`. ## Batch — cache Verify + Verdict phases of fast review (Unreleased)

> CW's dogfood audit measured the fast review's foreground phases: Verify ~146s and Verdict ~294s were the two most expensive AND the only ones not result-cached. They now cache like Map/Assess, so a warm re-run over an unchanged repo is near-instant instead of paying ~440s again.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Add `resultCache: sourceContextResultCache({ includeCompletedResults: "previous-phases" })` to the Verify (`verify:p0-p2-risks`) and Verdict (`verdict:fast-synthesis`) tasks of `apps/architecture-review-fast/workflow.js` (runtime P1, dogfood-audit + PROJECT_MEMORY "Next Run"). Live metrics from the self-audit: Map 270s, Assess 298s, **Verify 146s, Verdict 294s** — the latter two were uncached, so a re-run paid them in full. The cache key includes the source digest AND every upstream phase's result digest, so a hit only when source + all Map/Assess(/Verify) outputs are byte-identical; it busts on any upstream change and the cached result still passes worker-output validation (`requiresEvidence` stays enforced). This is the integrity-safe pattern (unlike the registry mtime fast-path the audit flagged, which is deliberately rejected to preserve tamper-evidence). | plugins/cool-workflow/apps/architecture-review-fast/workflow.js + plugins/cool-workflow/test/architecture-review-fast-phase-cache-smoke.js (new) + plugins/cool-workflow/test/architecture-review-fast-smoke.js (contract flip) + docs/project-index.md + ITERATION_LOG.md | New `architecture-review-fast-phase-cache-smoke` drives the FULL pipeline twice with a stub agent: cold run spawns every phase; warm run serves EVERY phase incl. Verify + Verdict from the result cache (no agent spawn). `architecture-review-fast-smoke` updated: Verify/Verdict now assert the cache config instead of "stays uncached". All 3 fast-review smokes green. | BUILD n/a (app JS, no dist); version:sync GREEN; onramp:check GREEN (apps change + smokes + log row); index:check GREEN | no (dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

## Batch — Homebrew install path (Unreleased)

> A user can now install `cw` with Homebrew, not only `npm install -g`. The formula lives inside the one `cool-workflow` repo (no separate `homebrew-*` tap repo), tapped by URL: `brew tap coo1white/cool-workflow https://github.com/coo1white/cool-workflow && brew install coo1white/cool-workflow/cool-workflow`. The formula is a tracked version surface, so it can never drift from the released tag.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Add a Homebrew install path that obeys the one-repo rule (no `homebrew-cool-workflow` tap repo). New `Formula/cool-workflow.rb` at the repo root is found by Homebrew tap discovery and tapped by URL, so the repo keeps its `cool-workflow` name. The formula pins `url ...cool-workflow.git, tag: "v<version>"` — a git-tag formula, so the git revision is the integrity pin and **no sha256** is needed; this drops the chicken-and-egg of a post-publish checksum and adds **zero** new release steps. Install `cd`s into `plugins/cool-workflow` and runs `npm install *std_npm_args` (committed `dist/`, zero runtime deps → no build, just pack + link the `cw`/`cool-workflow` bins); `depends_on "node"` covers `engines.node >=18`. Homebrew reads the version FROM the tag, so the tag is the single version surface: `scripts/bump-version.js` swaps only `tag: "v<old>"` on each bump, and `scripts/version-sync-check.js` asserts that tag at HEAD, exactly like the CHANGELOG/RELEASE checks (an explicit `version` line is redundant and `brew audit` rejects it). README gains a Homebrew block beside the npm one (then `sync:readme` regenerates the npm README); RELEASE.md notes the new auto-bumped surface. | Formula/cool-workflow.rb (new) + plugins/cool-workflow/scripts/bump-version.js + plugins/cool-workflow/scripts/version-sync-check.js + README.md (+ generated plugins/cool-workflow/README.md) + RELEASE.md + ITERATION_LOG.md | `version:sync` is the test: it now asserts `Formula/cool-workflow.rb` carries `tag: "v0.1.92"` — RED before the formula/scripts land (file/literal missing), GREEN after. Validated in a real Homebrew tap (not only by path): `brew info` loads the formula (stable 0.1.92, depends node, BSD-2-Clause), `brew style` reports no offenses under Homebrew's formula cops, and `brew audit` is clean once the redundant `version` line is dropped (the live audit caught it). | BUILD OK; version:sync GREEN (Formula tag assert included); readme:check GREEN; onramp:check GREEN (log row recorded) | no (dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

## Batch — test-harness agent-env hermeticity (Unreleased)

> The smoke runner now hands every smoke child a clean agent/backend slate, so `release-flow.js --cut` no longer false-fails at the deterministic gate when the reviewer is configured via the `CW_AGENT_COMMAND` env var.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Test-harness hermeticity — strip ambient agent/backend env from each smoke child. `run-all.js makeSandbox()` spread `...process.env` then set `CW_NO_AUTO_AGENT=1`, but that flag only blocks PATH auto-detection (`detectAgentFromPath`); an explicit `CW_AGENT_COMMAND`/`CW_AGENT_ENDPOINT` exported into the parent still resolved (flags > env > file, `agent-config.ts` `agentConfigFromEnv`), so fail-closed / no-agent / blocked smokes false-FAILED. That is exactly how `release-flow.js --cut` REJECTED at the deterministic gate (`release-gate.sh` → `CW_TEST_CONCURRENCY=1 npm test`, run in the SAME process env as the reviewer config) when the reviewer was set via the `CW_AGENT_COMMAND` env var — the var release-flow's own "no reviewer agent configured" hint suggests — even though `npm run release:check`, run without that env var, passed. Fix: `makeSandbox()` now deletes the whole ambient set (`CW_AGENT_COMMAND`/`CW_AGENT_ENDPOINT`/`CW_AGENT_MODEL`/`CW_AGENT_TIMEOUT_MS`/`CW_AGENT_ATTEST_PUBKEY`/`CW_AGENT_ATTEST_PRIVKEY`, `CW_REQUIRE_ATTESTED_TELEMETRY`, `CW_BACKEND`) from every smoke child's env — centralizing the scrub the ~20 agent-config-sensitive smokes each hand-rolled at their own top, and completing the intent of the earlier cycle that first added `CW_NO_AUTO_AGENT=1`. POLA: a no-op when none are set (the normal `npm test`); it only restores hermeticity when one IS exported. | plugins/cool-workflow/test/run-all.js + plugins/cool-workflow/test/run-all-agent-env-hermetic-smoke.js + plugins/cool-workflow/docs/project-index.md + ITERATION_LOG.md | new `run-all-agent-env-hermetic-smoke`: copies the self-contained run-all.js into a temp dir, drops a probe smoke, runs the copied runner with all 8 agent/backend vars PLUS a non-agent sentinel exported into its parent env; the probe asserts the agent set was stripped AND the sentinel survived (targeted scrub, non-vacuous). Fails-before confirmed (unpatched runner ⇒ probe sees all 8 leaked ⇒ FAIL); passes-after. End-to-end: `CW_AGENT_COMMAND="claude -p {{prompt}}" CW_TEST_CONCURRENCY=1 npm test` — the literal gate test step — now stays green. | BUILD OK; onramp:check OK (ok:true; test-only ⇒ no log row required, added for discipline); release-flow-smoke OK under the harness-equivalent env; full gate test step (CW_AGENT_COMMAND set) green | no (test-infra fix; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

## Batch — orchestration capability parity (Unreleased)

> Four capabilities ported from the harness Workflow tool into CW's engine, each its own dev-loop cycle, each additive + opt-in + replay-deterministic: (1) incremental resume, (2) inline sub-workflow nesting, (3) bounded dynamic control flow, (4) budget-aware scaling.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 4 | Budget-aware SCALING — turn the fail-closed token-budget CAP into adaptive depth. Extends PR-3's loop engine with a `budget-target` arm on `LoopUntil` (`loop("Scale", tasks, {maxRounds, until:{kind:"budget-target", target}})`): after each round, `maybeExpandLoop` evaluates `done = deriveUsageTotals(run).totals.totalTokens >= target` (RECORDED, the same usage the cap reads) and spawns another round while under target, hard-capped at `maxRounds`. COMPOSES with the existing fail-closed cap (`drive.ts` enforces `limits.tokenBudget` before each spawn): whichever fires first wins, and the cap remains the ABSOLUTE backstop — it can never be overshot. Pure reuse of PR-3's engine (the decision dispatch grows a `kind==="budget-target"` arm; everything else — expansion, loop-control nodes, the static `maxLoopExpansion` bound, POLA — is unchanged). Deterministic (decision is a pure function of recorded usage). | src/types/workflow-app.ts (`LoopUntil` gains the `budget-target` arm) + src/orchestrator/lifecycle-operations.ts (`maybeExpandLoop` decision dispatch) + src/workflow-api.ts (`loop()` accepts budget-target) + src/workflow-app-framework.ts (validate the arm) + dist/* + test/budget-scaling-loop-smoke.js + docs/project-index.md | new `budget-scaling-loop-smoke`: target 18 + 6 tokens/hop ⇒ exactly 3 rounds (6,12,18 reaches the target; round 4 not spawned), completes; with `limits.tokenBudget:12` ALSO set, the CAP fires first — the run BLOCKS after 2 rounds (spent 12 >= budget 12), proving the cap is the absolute backstop. Fails-before confirmed (neuter the budget-target decision → loop runs to maxRounds 8, "exactly 3 rounds" fails). Regression smokes (loop / canonical-workflow-apps / token-budget-enforcement) green; source-context guard fits. | BUILD OK; dist:check OK; index:check OK; onramp:check OK; parity OK; schema OK; version:sync OK; (full suite pending) | no (capability cycle; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |
| 3 | Bounded DYNAMIC CONTROL FLOW — a declarative `loop(name, tasks, {maxRounds, until})` phase whose tasks are a per-round TEMPLATE: after each round completes, a registered PURE predicate decides whether to run another round (a fresh phase appended right after, same tasks under `@r{n}`-suffixed ids) or stop, hard-capped at `maxRounds`. Engine `maybeExpandLoop(run)` hooks the accept path (`recordWorkerOutput` after `updatePhaseStatuses`, before `commitState`): when a loop's LATEST round-phase's tasks all complete, it gathers their results in `compareBytes` id order, evaluates the predicate, RECORDS the decision as a deterministic `loop-control` StateNode (the replay source of truth), then either marks the ORIGIN phase `loopDone` (decision done OR `round>=maxRounds`) or clones the round-1 template tasks into a new phase + materializes them (writeTaskFiles + the `plan` pipeline stage). DETERMINISM: predicate is a NAMED registry ref (not an inline closure); decision is a pure function of RECORDED results + recorded usage; an unregistered predicate stops fail-closed. BOUND: the drive's `maxIterations` adds `maxLoopExpansion(run0)` = Σ`(maxRounds-1)·templateTasks` — derived STATICALLY from the declaration, so the drive provably terminates; `plannedWorkers` stays the initial count (status unchanged). POLA: every field optional; `maybeExpandLoop` no-ops without a loop phase; `maxLoopExpansion`=0 ⇒ the bound reduces to today's. | src/loop-expansion.ts (new: registry + built-ins + `maxLoopExpansion`) + src/orchestrator/lifecycle-operations.ts (`maybeExpandLoop` + accept hook + `plan`/`flattenTasks` carry loop+loopRound) + src/drive.ts (`maxIterations` static expansion) + src/workflow-api.ts (`loop()` builder) + src/types/{workflow-app,result,run,core}.ts (additive loop fields + `loop-control` kind) + src/workflow-app-framework.ts (validate) + dist/* + test/loop-bounded-expansion-smoke.js + docs/project-index.md | new `loop-bounded-expansion-smoke`: maxRounds:5 + stop-at-round-3 predicate runs EXACTLY 3 rounds (3 phases/tasks/loop-control nodes, origin loopDone) then completes; a never-done predicate stops at the CAP (round 2 via atCap); `maxLoopExpansion`=4; a result node replays byte-identically under two `now`; two runs under different `now` produce the IDENTICAL recorded decision sequence. Fails-before confirmed (neuter `maybeExpandLoop` → "exactly 3 rounds ran" fails). Regression smokes (det-ids / canonical-workflow-apps / agent-delegation-drive / sub-workflow-nesting) green; source-context guard fits. | BUILD OK; dist:check OK; index:check OK; onramp:check OK; parity OK; schema OK; version:sync OK; (full suite pending) | no (capability cycle; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |
| 2 | Inline SUB-WORKFLOW nesting — a task fulfilled by planning + driving a CHILD app run instead of spawning an agent directly; the child's report (or verdict result) is bound back as the task's result, so the parent's verifier/schema/evidence gate + downstream tasks consume it like any other result. A new fulfillment branch in `processSelectedTask` (after PR-A's cache-miss check, before `runBackend` — orthogonal) plans a child with a DETERMINISTIC id `sub-<parentRunId>-<taskId>` (no clock/random; `plan()` now honors an injected `runId`, stripped from `run.inputs` so digests stay clean — POLA) and recursively `drive()`s it. Child inputs are rendered from the PARENT inputs (`{{key}}` templates) → deterministic; default inherits repo/cwd/question. HONESTY: the parent does NOT sum/fabricate child telemetry — it records ONE `worker.sub-workflow` trust-audit cross-link (child run id + `sha256(childBytes)` + the child's own `verifyTrustAudit` verdict), and the task points at the child run dir (`subRunId`/`subRunDir`); the child's usage/audit stay in the child run. FAIL-CLOSED bounded recursion: `MAX_SUB_WORKFLOW_DEPTH=4` + a `visitedAppIds` cycle guard (includes the current app, so a direct self-cycle A→A is refused at depth 0 before any child dir is minted); a child that does not complete parks the parent hop. Leaf work is still external-agent delegation at every level (no model SDK imported at any depth). | src/drive.ts (subWorkflow branch + `runSubWorkflow`/`renderSubInputs` + DriveOptions/DriveContext depth/visitedAppIds + `MAX_SUB_WORKFLOW_DEPTH`) + src/orchestrator/lifecycle-operations.ts (`plan` injected runId; `flattenTasks` carries subWorkflow) + src/workflow-api.ts (`subWorkflow()` builder) + src/types/{workflow-app,run}.ts (additive `subWorkflow`/`subRunId`/`subRunDir`) + dist/* + test/sub-workflow-nesting-smoke.js + docs/project-index.md | new `sub-workflow-nesting-smoke` (temp fixture apps via `runner.appsDir`): parent delegates to a child (handleKind "sub-workflow"), a downstream parent task consumes it, both run; child run nested-id'd + recorded on the task; ONE honest `worker.sub-workflow` cross-link; `verifyTrustAudit` passes for BOTH chains; the sub-workflow result node replays byte-identically under two `now`; a self-cycle app and a chain deeper than the depth cap each PARK fail-closed. Fails-before confirmed (neuter the branch → "fulfilled by a sub-workflow" fails, the stub agent fulfills it instead). Regression smokes (det-ids / canonical-workflow-apps / agent-delegation-drive / incremental-resume) green. | BUILD OK; dist:check OK; index:check OK; onramp:check OK; parity OK; schema OK; version:sync OK; (full suite pending) | no (capability cycle; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |
| 1 | `cw run --drive --incremental` — automatic step-level incremental resume. Generalizes the existing OPT-IN content-addressed result cache (`drive.ts resultCachePath`) into a run-level mode that keys EVERY task by `{rendered prompt + full run.inputs + upstream-result digests}` (`schemaVersion:2`, never colliding with the opt-in `schemaVersion:1`). The phase barrier guarantees upstream tasks are completed before a task runs, so their result bytes are digestible; a changed prompt/input perturbs that task's key and its changed result perturbs every downstream key → the longest unchanged prefix replays from cache (0 agent spawns) and the first changed task + everything downstream re-runs. Chose to key on upstream RESULT bytes (not the plan's prompt-only chain): a CW prompt is rendered from run.inputs and does NOT carry an upstream task's result, so a nondeterministic/edited upstream result must invalidate downstream — only result-byte keying does that. `--incremental` added to `DRIVE_RUNTIME_KEYS` so it never poisons `run.inputs`/the key. Honesty: a reused hop reuses the EXISTING cache-hit accept path (no fabricated usage; surfaced as `handleKind:"result-cache"`), matching the already-reviewed opt-in cache semantics. **Adversarial review (4 lenses) confirmed 2 fixed before merge:** (MAJOR false-reuse + honesty) the key omitted the per-task DELEGATION config — `task.model`, `task.agentType`, `task.sandboxProfileId`, AND the resolved global `--agent-model` (stripped from run.inputs) — so editing the model/backend/sandbox served a STALE result that ALSO attested the wrong model; fixed by folding an `incrementalDelegationDigest` (resolved model + agentType + stable sandbox PROFILE ID — the id, not the full policy, whose paths embed the per-run worker dir and would defeat all reuse) into the key; (MINOR) the smoke never exercised the concurrent driver — added a `concurrency:6` reuse scenario. **A second review round** confirmed one more (MINOR, honestly judged) vector of the same class: the agent IDENTITY (`config.command`/`args`/`endpoint` — operator flags also stripped from run.inputs) selects WHICH binary/endpoint produces the bytes, so swapping the agent with the model held constant false-reused; folded `command` + secret-stripped `args` + `endpoint` into the delegation digest and added an agent-swap smoke scenario. The completeness lens cleared the remaining candidates (task.schema is re-validated at intake; timeoutMs/label/retryCount are not result-determining). | src/drive.ts (incremental key + `incrementalDelegationDigest` + `previousPhaseResultsDigest` extract + branch + DriveOptions/DriveContext) + src/capability-core.ts (thread `incremental`, DRIVE_RUNTIME_KEYS) + src/cli/command-surface.ts (usage) + dist/* + test/incremental-resume-smoke.js + docs/project-index.md | new `incremental-resume-smoke` (7 scenarios): reuse across runs (0 re-spawns, every accept `result-cache`), byte-identical reused bytes, NON-incremental re-runs everything (POLA), per-task granularity, downstream invalidation (Map prefix reused, later phases re-run), MODEL SWAP + AGENT-IDENTITY SWAP each invalidate every entry (no false reuse), concurrent-driver reuse. Fails-before confirmed twice (neuter the incremental branch → "run 2 spawns ZERO" fails; neuter the delegation digest → "model swap... no false reuse" fails). Opt-in-cache + determinism regression smokes green. | BUILD OK; dist:check OK; index:check OK; onramp:check OK; parity:check OK; validate:schema OK; version:sync OK; test:fast 124/124 | no (capability cycle; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

## Batch - Security hardening: archive import path traversal (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |

| 1 | Close a path-traversal (zip-slip class) in run-archive import. `importRun` trusted `raw.run.id` straight into `path.join(target, ".cw/runs", id)`, so a crafted bundle with `run.id` = `../../../escape` wrote a run tree OUTSIDE `<target>/.cw/runs/` — before any digest gate could matter — and `cw report verify-bundle` (the "safe inspect" command that restores an untrusted bundle into a throwaway tmpdir) inherited the same escape, writing outside the tmpdir while still returning `ok:true`. Adversarial review (workflow): ship-with-followups, 0 blockers — folded all 4 followups in (narrowed `..`, verify-bundle assertion, legible accept-case, before-write ordering assert). | src/state.ts (assertSafeRunId) + src/run-export.ts (importRun guard) + dist/{state,run-export}.js + test/run-import-path-traversal-smoke.js + docs/run-registry-control-plane.7.md + docs/project-index.md | new `run-import-path-traversal-smoke`: a traversal `run.id` is refused before any directory is created (no escaped dir/file; target runs tree stays empty); `cw report verify-bundle` surfaces the same refusal as a failed `restore` check (`ok:false`) without tmpdir escape; a benign id still imports (POLA round-trip); `assertSafeRunId` unit table (10 reject / 7 accept incl. embedded-`..` ids). Fails-before/passes-after confirmed (guard neutered → importRun no longer threw / escaped tree appeared). Export/import regression smokes green; full suite 120/120. | BUILD OK; check OK; dist:check OK (dist matches src); gen:manifests OK; index:check OK; onramp:check OK; release:check all green; test:fast 120/120 | no (security hardening cycle; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

## Batch - Runtime: appendRunNode O(N^2) array churn (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Remove an O(N^2) allocation on the kernel's hottest write path. `appendRunNode` ran on every dispatch/result/blackboard node append and rebuilt the WHOLE `run.nodes` array each call (`index >= 0 ? nodes.map(...) : [...nodes, node]`), so a run that appends N nodes allocated arrays of size 1..N — O(N^2) memory churn + GC. Replaced with in-place mutation (`nodes[index] = node` for upsert, `nodes.push(node)` for a new node), keeping the array content AND order byte-identical so persisted `state.json` is unchanged. `appendRunNode` is the only writer of `run.nodes`, so the array reference is now stable across a run. | src/state-node.ts + dist/state-node.js + test/append-run-node-no-realloc-smoke.js + docs/project-index.md | new `append-run-node-no-realloc-smoke`: reference stability across appends (the behavioral fails-before — the old realloc reassigned `run.nodes`, verified by reverting the compiled body), insertion-order preservation, in-place upsert (same array/length/slot, replaced content), persistence via writeRunNode unchanged, and missing-`nodes` initialization. Node-heavy regression smokes (state-node, blackboard-state-explosion, node-snapshot-diff-replay) green. Full suite 120/120. | BUILD OK; check OK; dist:check OK; index:check OK; onramp:check OK; test:fast 120/120 | no (runtime cycle; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

The lone ed25519 signature only ever covered the agent's reported USAGE (`canonicalTelemetryPayload`), never the CW-rendered report — so that sentence was false. Re-scoped to the currently-true claim (reusing the honest framing already in `docs/trust-model.md`): usage is signed; the record is hash-chained / tamper-evident; the report text itself is NOT signed; CW holds no key. Added a Trust Model pointer. (PRs 2-4 add real result+report crypto coverage, then strengthen the wording back up.) | README.md + test/readme-trust-claim-smoke.js + docs/project-index.md | new `readme-trust-claim-smoke`: the trust section drops the false "signature no longer matches" / "Every agent step is recorded, signed" wording and carries the honest scoping (usage-scoped signature, "report text itself is not signed", "no private key") + a working Trust Model link. Fails-before against main's README; passes-after. Full suite green. | index:check OK; dist:check OK; onramp:check OK; test:fast green | no (docs honesty cycle; dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

At intake `worker-accept/telemetry-ledger.ts` passes `resultDigest = sha256(rawResult)` (the SAME raw bytes the executor signed; CW's and the wrapper's `sha256` are identical impls) into the verify ctx; `verifyTelemetryAttestation` tries the result-bound payload, falling back to the 4-field one for back-compat — so a NEW signer whose result is edited fails BOTH arms (tamper caught), while old signatures still verify. The INDEPENDENT re-verifier (`verifyTelemetrySignatures`, behind `telemetry verify --pubkey` / `report verify` / restore) must also accept result-bound signatures: the signed `resultDigest` is now stored on the hash-chained ledger record (chain-bound, present only when the signature covered it — so usage-only records stay byte-identical) and threaded into the re-verify ctx. | 4 | Strengthen the wording to the now-true guarantee + make `cw demo tamper` prove it (PR 4 of 4). Now that PR2 signs the result and PR3 cross-checks it, the root README "Can I Trust the Report?" is rewritten from the PR1 honest-but-narrow claim ("usage is signed; the report text is not signed") up to the now-true FORWARD claim: the agent **signs its findings** (ed25519) and `cw report verify-bundle` confirms — offline, public key only — every signed finding is in the report **unaltered** (edit a finding, in the report or the agent's own result, and the check fails); CW holds **no private key**. Kept the honest forward-scope caveat ("not that the report holds nothing else") + the Trust Model link, so the strengthened claim does not become the new overclaim. `cw demo tamper` gains a THIRD layer — a RESULT tamper: sign a result-COVERING (5-field) ed25519 payload, then edit the signed finding; CW re-derives `sha256(result)` so the signature joins neither the 5-field nor the 4-field payload and the verify rejects it — so the README's "edits a signed result, watch it fail" is demonstrable, not just asserted. Synced the launch-kit copy ("two ways" → "three ways (incl. editing a signed finding)") to match the demo. **Adversarial review (4 lenses, 7 confirmed) folded in:** (a) HONESTY (major) — the strengthened README forward claim "every signed finding is in the report unaltered" carried only the EXTRA-CONTENT caveat ("not that the report holds nothing else") and was silent on OMISSION, so a careful reader could read the universal "every" as a completeness guarantee — the dropped converse. Per such record: a present completed task whose RESTORED result hashes to the signed digest (`result-missing`/`result-digest-mismatch`) + `report.md` embeds it at the task's own `### <taskId>` section, body-first walking occurrences (`report-result-mismatch`). (b) **Close the fail-open**: `trustLevel` "signed" requires a result-covering signature re-verified + the forward check held (`resultBound>0 && failed===0 && reportFindingsOk` — a usage-only 4-field bundle is "unsigned") + `--require-signatures` refusing an unsigned bundle. Fixed the FORWARD half robustly (record-driven iteration + `coversResult` + occurrence-walking). **The converse ("report presents only signed findings") was DROPPED — user decision** — as not robustly achievable here (no key to sign the rendered report; self-recomputable chain). Every attack repro fails against the flawed cuts, passes against the fix. Real signed runs (architecture-review/golden-path/dogfood) verify unchanged. ## Batch - Test hermeticity: release-flow smoke runs bare (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Make `release-flow-smoke.js` self-hermetic so it is correct whether run bare (`node test/release-flow-smoke.js`) or under `run-all.js`'s sandbox. The `runFlow(dir, { agentCmd })` helper built the child env without `CW_NO_AUTO_AGENT=1` and without overriding `CW_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME`. For Case 4 ("no agent configured") it only `delete`d `CW_AGENT_COMMAND`/`CW_AGENT_ENDPOINT`, so `resolveAgentConfig` fell through to `detectAgentFromPath`, auto-detected a real agent CLI on PATH (claude/codex/gemini — as in a dev box), resolved `builtin:<name>`, and release-flow spawned it. The fail-closed assertion then saw `reviewer agent exited (timeout/no-exit)` instead of the expected `no reviewer agent configured`. The smoke passed only under `npm test` because `run-all.js` sandboxes each child with `CW_NO_AUTO_AGENT=1` + a throwaway `CW_HOME`. Now `runFlow` always sets `CW_NO_AUTO_AGENT=1` and points `CW_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME` at a throwaway `<fixture>/.cw-home`, so neither PATH auto-detection nor a durable host `agent-config.json` can configure an agent — the agent layers under test are flags/env ONLY. ## Batch - release-gate hotfix path (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |

| 1 | The deterministic release gate's cadence check (`>=4 cycle-lines OR >=24h since the prior tag`) has no path for an urgent hotfix: 0.1.88 shipped with a broken headline `cw -q` and the fix (#244) needs to go out well inside the 24h window. Add an EXPLICIT, RECORDED hotfix bypass to `release-gate.sh`: when cadence is otherwise unmet, the gate looks for a `HOTFIX:` line added to `ITERATION_LOG.md` in the release range, and only then passes cadence — echoing the reason (`cadence bypassed by recorded HOTFIX: …`). The bypass is committed (auditable in the tag's history) and printed (a reviewer sees the reason), never silent. ## Batch - Review a remote repo by URL (`--link`) (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |

| 1 | Review a REMOTE git repo by URL: `cw -q "…" --link <url>` (and a URL passed to `-dir`/`--repo`) clones the remote into a local cache, then runs the EXISTING review pipeline against the checkout. Cloning is non-deterministic network I/O, so it lives in the CAPABILITY layer (`quickstart()`), above the cwd-default and before `plan` — the replay-deterministic core only ever sees a local path. New zero-dep `remote-source.ts` shells out to `git clone --depth 1 --single-branch -c core.hooksPath= -- <url> <dir>` with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`/`GIT_ASKPASS=`/`GCM_INTERACTIVE=never` (fail closed on auth, never hangs), array-argv + `--` (no shell), a scheme ALLOWLIST (https/http/ssh/git/file; rejects `ext::`/`fd::` helpers and `-`-leading option-injection), and credential sanitization (userinfo stripped before any cache-key/print/persist/provenance, AND git's own stderr/stdout is credential-redacted before surfacing — a defense-in-depth layer an adversarial security review flagged, since git can echo a credentialed URL on auth failure). Content-addressed cache under `resolveCwHome()/clones/<hash>` with reuse + `--refresh`. Provenance is tamper-evident: the sanitized `url@<sha>` rides into `run.inputs` → a `Source:` line in report.md, the `--json` result `remote{…}`, AND a hash-chained `source.clone` trust-audit event (`cw audit verify` re-proves it; metadata auto-scrubbed of credentials). `--check` validates URL shape + git availability WITHOUT fetching. | src/remote-source.ts (new: classify/validate/sanitize/cloneGit/materialize), src/capability-core.ts (materialize seam in quickstart + remoteQuickstartCheck + DRIVE_RUNTIME_KEYS link/ref/branch/refresh + source.clone event + stamp sourceUrl/Commit/Ref + QuickstartResult.remote), src/orchestrator.ts (shortMap `l:"link"` + formatHelp `--link` example), src/orchestrator/report.ts (conditional `Source:` line), src/types/drive.ts (QuickstartResult.remote), README.md + plugins/cool-workflow/README.md (--link section), manifest/source-context-profiles.json + test/source-context-profile-smoke.js (runtime source-context guard 44k→48k as the kernel grew with the new module), docs/project-index.md, dist/ regenerated, ITERATION_LOG.md | `remote-link-git-smoke` (new) is hermetic + offline (a local bare repo via a `file://` URL, stub agent — no network, no live model): `--check` passes WITHOUT creating a clone; a real run clones + reviews + `remote.commit===bare HEAD`, report.md has `Source: url@sha`, and the audit log holds a `source.clone` event with that sha; the second run reuses the cache (`cached:true`, one entry); `-dir <url>` auto-detects identically; and every fail-closed path holds — unreachable `file://` and blocked `ext::` exit non-zero with no run, and a `user:SECRET@host` URL exits non-zero with the credential appearing NOWHERE under the cache/home. | BUILD OK; onramp:check; remote-link-git-smoke; CLI/MCP parity; full suite (134 smokes) | no (dev loop; ships as 0.1.91 via the gated cut) |
| 2 | Extend `--link` to downloadable ARCHIVES (`.tar.gz`/`.tgz`/`.tar`/`.zip`, e.g. a GitHub "Download ZIP"/codeload link). `materializeRemote` now dispatches an archive URL to `downloadArchive`: fetch the bytes (a `file://` is read directly; http(s) is fetched by a short `node -e` subprocess using Node's built-in `fetch` — zero deps — run synchronously via spawnSync since the CLI flow is synchronous; size-capped at 200 MiB), content-address by sha256 of the bytes (= the `commit`, since there is no git SHA), then extract with a path-traversal guard and snapshot into a local git repo so the EXISTING source-context reader (which reads only git-tracked blobs) works unchanged. Security/fail-closed carries over: a zip-slip/tar-slip entry (absolute path or `..`) is REJECTED by validating the archive listing (`tar -tf`/`unzip -Z1`) BEFORE extracting; extraction descends into a single top-level wrapper dir (GitHub style); `unzip` absence on a `.zip` fails closed with an actionable message; download errors are credential-redacted. Provenance records a `source.download` trust-audit event (already wired by kind in PR 1). An adversarial security review fleet (26 agents) then verified several real exploits in the extraction path, all HARDENED before merge: (a) a single top-level SYMLINK passed the `fs.statSync` wrapper-descent (follows links) and landed in the cache → switched to `fs.lstatSync` + a post-extraction tree walk that rejects ANY symlink/non-regular entry (version-independent symlink-traversal defense); (b) the http(s) fetch followed redirects with `redirect:'follow'`, an SSRF vector → now follows redirects MANUALLY and re-validates each hop (http(s) scheme + no private/loopback/link-local host) BEFORE connecting; (c) unbounded extraction (zip bomb) → reject by DECLARED uncompressed size (gzip ISIZE / `unzip -l` total) before extracting AND by ACTUAL size (1 GiB cap) after; (d) a deterministic temp-download path raced concurrent same-URL runs → temp file now lives in the mkdtemp staging dir (unique); (e) tightened the unzip-missing detection (ENOENT, not a stderr regex). | src/remote-source.ts (downloadArchive + fetchArchiveBytes [SSRF manual-redirect] + listArchive + assertNoTraversal + assertSafeTree [symlink/size walk] + declaredUncompressedSize [bomb] + gitSnapshot; MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES/MAX_EXTRACTED_BYTES; os/url imports), README.md + plugins/cool-workflow/README.md (archive note), docs/project-index.md, dist/ regenerated, ITERATION_LOG.md | `remote-link-archive-smoke` (new) is hermetic + offline: a `.tar.gz` (and a `.zip` when zip/unzip are present) built locally and addressed via `file://` downloads → extracts → snapshots → reviews, with `remote.kind==="archive"`, `remote.commit===sha256(archive bytes)`, a `Source: url@<contentSha>` report line, a `source.download` audit event, and cache reuse; a hand-forged ustar tar whose entry is `../evil-escaped.txt` is REJECTED (name traversal); a forged SYMLINK entry (plain + single-top-level-to-dir) is REJECTED with no write-through; a gzip with a forged huge ISIZE is REJECTED as a bomb before extraction; and a loopback HTTP server that 302-redirects to a private host is BLOCKED (SSRF) via async spawn. | BUILD OK; onramp:check; remote-link-archive-smoke; CLI/MCP parity; full suite (135 smokes) | no (dev loop; ships as 0.1.91 via the gated cut) |
| 3 | Manage the `--link` clone cache: `cw clones list` (every cached checkout — origin url, kind, commit, age, bytes) and `cw clones gc [--older-than-days N] [--all]` (a TTL sweep, default 30 days, or reclaim everything). Pure filesystem work in a new zero-dep `clones.ts` (no network, no run registry); `gc` deletes ONLY paths it proves are inside the clones root (containment, fail closed). A focused adversarial review (no deletion-escape found — the symlink-hash-dir + containment are safe) drove three fail-SAFE hardenings: a TTL sweep now KEEPS an undateable (no-meta/partial) entry instead of deleting it as "age 0" (only `--all` clears those), a negative `--older-than-days` and an unparseable `--now` are REJECTED with a clear error (never a future-cutoff or NaN that silently mis-deletes). ## Batch - 0.1.90 release cut (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |

| 1 | Cut **0.1.90** — the golden-path polish (`-dir`, live progress, clean summary, recoverable errors) + the whole "review any repo by URL" feature (`--link` git + archive, `cw clones`, tamper-evident provenance). Release-prep: `bump:version --content` (code surfaces + the 22 doc/README/RELEASE content sections), a `## 0.1.90` CHANGELOG entry, and a regenerated `docs/project-index.md`. An AUDIT (instead of reactively re-running the ~30-min gated cut) surfaced three latent release-prep traps, now fixed and recorded: (a) `bump:version` does NOT run `sync:project-index`, so the index's `Version:` field went stale → `project-index-sync-smoke` failed; (b) the gitignored `package-lock.json` had drifted (nested 0.1.87), breaking `version-sync-check` — deleted it (`checkJsonIfPresent` skips an absent lock; install uses `--no-package-lock`); (c) tsc's incremental cache (`.cache/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo`) silently kept `dist/version.js` at 0.1.89 even after the bump, so `cw version` reported the old number and `dist:check` didn't catch it — `rm -rf .cache dist && npm run build` fixes it. Process change: run `npm run release:check` to FULL GREEN locally BEFORE `--cut` (it is the cut's gate without the 15-min reviewer, so it surfaces every failure in one ~14-min local pass), and after a version bump regenerate ALL version-embedding generated artifacts, not just manifests+dist. | bump: package.json + src/version.ts + apps/*/app.json + manifest + the version-referencing scripts/tests; content: README.md + plugins/cool-workflow/README.md + docs/*.7.md + RELEASE.md; CHANGELOG.md; docs/project-index.md (regenerated); dist/ (clean rebuild); ITERATION_LOG.md | `release:check` green locally (full smoke suite 136 + dist-drift + type-check + onramp + parity + manifests + version-sync + project-index-sync) is the pre-cut validation; the gated cut then re-runs `release-gate.sh` + an independent `claude -p` reviewer that re-certifies it. | release:check GREEN; gated release-flow --cut --push (gate + reviewer APPROVED) | yes — v0.1.90 (gated cut, signed tag, npm publish via OIDC) |

## Batch - Calm, Claude-Code-style live terminal output (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |

| 1 | Make `cw`'s live run output feel like Claude Code / Codex CLI — a calm, progressively-updating view instead of an append-only wall — PRESENTATION ONLY (the orchestration model, ed25519 signing/tamper-evidence, and the `cw:result` schema are untouched; stdout stays byte-exact). **Layer 1 (the live AGENT view)** lives in the async per-vendor wrapper, which already parses `--output-format stream-json` and whose event loop is free (cw is blocked in `spawnSync` mid-agent, so cw cannot animate — confirmed Architecture A). A hand-rolled, zero-dep renderer in the shared vendor-neutral `agent-adapter-core.js` (`createRenderer`) draws ONE in-place status line (Braille spinner + current action + elapsed), folds each tool call to a single `✓ Read app.js (0.3s)` / `✗ Bash (1.1s)` line (width-aware truncate), hides the cursor while the spinner runs and ALWAYS restores it on exit/SIGINT/SIGTERM (Ctrl-C → exit 130, clean terminal). Non-TTY stays SILENT by default (Rule of Silence); `CW_AGENT_STREAM=1` opts a CI/pipe into a plain append-only trace (zero ANSI). ## Batch - 0.1.91 release cut (Unreleased)

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Cut **0.1.91** — the calm, Claude-Code-style live terminal output (Layer 1 live agent view in the wrappers + Layer 2 `reporter.ts` + `--verbose`/`--no-color`/`--full`, zero new deps, presentation-only). Release-prep: a real `## 0.1.91` CHANGELOG entry (Capability/Implementation/Tests/Risk), `bump:version --content` (numeric surfaces + the ~21 doc/RELEASE version refs), and a regenerated `docs/project-index.md`. Re-hit and recorded the three known release-prep traps (all handled): (a) the plain `bump:version` the cut runs only VERIFIES content surfaces reference the new version, so the cut fails closed until the `## 0.1.91` CHANGELOG + doc refs exist — write content BEFORE the cut; (b) the gitignored `package-lock.json` had drifted to 0.1.91 while `version-sync-check` reads the canonical version from HEAD (`git show HEAD:package.json` = 0.1.90 pre-commit), so the working-tree lock was the only non-HEAD surface and mismatched — deleted it (`checkJsonIfPresent` skips an absent lock); (c) `bump:version` rebuilds dist but a stale `.cache/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` can keep `dist/version.js` at the old version — `rm -rf .cache dist && npm run build` before the cut. The gated cut (`release-flow --cut --version 0.1.91`) then re-runs the deterministic gate + an independent `claude -p` reviewer (verdict `APPROVED <prep-sha>`) and produces the verdict commit + signed tag; `--push` (or a manual push) ships the tag and triggers the provenance-attested npm publish via CI OIDC. | CHANGELOG.md (## 0.1.91), RELEASE.md + README.md + ~18 docs/*.7.md (version refs via bump:version --content), package.json + src/version.ts + apps/*/app.json + manifest + plugin.json surfaces + version-referencing scripts/tests, docs/project-index.md (regenerated), dist/ (clean rebuild), ITERATION_LOG.md | `release:check` FULL GREEN locally on the prep commit (full smoke suite + dist-drift + type-check + onramp + CLI/MCP parity + manifests + version-sync + project-index-sync) is the pre-cut validation; the gated cut then re-runs `release-gate.sh` + the independent reviewer that re-certifies the tagged commit. | release:check GREEN; gated release-flow --cut (gate + reviewer APPROVED) → push tag → CI npm publish | yes — v0.1.91 (gated cut, signed tag, npm publish via OIDC) |

## Batch - guard the -d/-dir/--dir == --repo on-disk contract (Unreleased)

> The README says `-d` / `--dir` / `--repo` are equivalent ways to point `cw` at a project folder. The wiring already exists (shortMap `d -> dir`, `command-surface` maps `dir -> repo`, with explicit `--repo` winning) and `headline-commands-smoke` §7 already drives all three aliases from a foreign cwd — but it only asserts the `--check` RESULT's `repo` field. Nothing drove a real run and proved the persisted `state.json` lands under the target. That on-disk layer is exactly the failure a user hit: run state created under their shell cwd's `.cw` (orphaned) instead of the project. This cycle locks that layer. No production change — POLA: the behavior is correct; the gap was a missing guard.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Close the one un-guarded layer of the documented `-d` / `-dir` / `--dir` == `--repo` contract. Verified first that the feature itself is already complete in this branch (shipped in `df919c3` #246, after the bug report was written against `main`): `parseArgv` shortMap maps `d -> dir` and `l -> link` (`src/orchestrator.ts`), `runCli` maps `dir -> repo` with explicit `--repo` winning (`src/cli/command-surface.ts`), and the `-q` headline consumes `positionals[0]` into the question so it is never read as the app id. Drove all four forms (`-d` / `-dir` / `--dir` / `--repo`) through the REAL CLI `cw plan` from a DIFFERENT cwd and confirmed `state.cwd === target`, `statePath` under `target/.cw`, and NO orphan `.cw` under the invocation cwd. The existing `headline-commands-smoke` §7 covers the same aliases but only at the `--check` repo-field layer; this adds §8, which drives a real (agent-free, deterministic) `cw plan research-synthesis <flag> <target>` from a foreign cwd and asserts the persisted run's `cwd` and state path land under the target with no cwd orphan — the precise "run.cwd === target" the report asked for. Red/green proven: with the `dir -> repo` line disabled and dist rebuilt, the alias contract goes RED (AssertionError on `-dir`); restored, GREEN. Secondary README claims re-verified by hand: `cw -q "q" --app <app>` plans `<app>` (6 workers), bare `cw -q "q"` defaults the app and never reads the question as an app id, and a URL passed to `-dir`/`--repo` is detected as a remote source in `quickstart` (same path as `--link`). | test/headline-commands-smoke.js (new §8: real-`plan` on-disk cwd guard over `-d`/`-dir`/`--dir`/`--repo` from a foreign cwd), ITERATION_LOG.md | `headline-commands-smoke` §8: `cw plan research-synthesis <flag> <target> --question … --json` from an unrelated cwd → exit 0, `statePath` startsWith target, `state.cwd === target`, no `<elsewhere>/.cw` orphan, for all four documented forms. §7 (the `--check` repo-field layer) and `cli-arg-parsing-smoke` (`-d` -> `dir` parse) stay green as the other two layers of the same contract. | BUILD OK; headline-commands-smoke GREEN; full suite | no (dev loop; test-only hardening) |

## Batch — add fail-closed `cw run restore <archive>` (inspect+import+verify in one step) (Unreleased)

> New user-facing verb that restores an exported run on another machine in ONE fail-closed step. The real gap: `run import` DOES run a verification (importRun returns it as `ImportResult.verification` — re-proving restored file digests, the telemetry ledger, AND the trust-audit hash chain) but does NOT fail on it — it exits 0 even when that chain does not verify. So a run whose telemetry/trust-audit chain was tampered (yet whose file digests are intact) imports with a made-up success. `run restore` shuts that gap: it integrity-INSPECTS the archive FIRST (writing nothing), refuses a bad bundle before any write, then IMPORTS and REUSES importRun's verification verdict — reporting `ok:true` ONLY when that verify passes. Track B (export -> restore) + fail-closed (a core project value). POLA: brand-new verb; `run import` / `run verify-import` / `run inspect-archive` stay byte-identical. Thin composition of the existing functions (`inspectArchive` + `importRun`, REUSING its verification — NO second re-hash) — no new crypto or IO. Wired on BOTH surfaces (CLI `run restore`, MCP `cw_run_restore`); parity green.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Add NEW capability `cw run restore <archive>` — fail-closed atomic restore (inspect -> import -> verify; refuses unverifiable bundles), closing the gap that `run import` skips verification. Core: `runRestoreArchive(runner, args)` in `src/capability-core.ts` mirrors `runImportArchive` for base/archive/target resolve (same `archive\|path\|file` aliases, same "requires an archive path" throw), then `inspectArchive` FIRST (read-only) — on `!inspect.ok` returns `{ ok:false, imported:null, verify:null, registry:null }` and imports NOTHING (no partial restore) — else `importRun` + repo-registry refresh + `verifyImportedRun` on the loaded imported run; `ok = inspect.ok && verify.ok`. New `RunRestoreResult` type `{ schemaVersion, ok, target, inspect, imported, verify, registry }` (scriptable). Wired BOTH surfaces (parity gate): registry row `run.restore` next to `run.inspect-archive` (`surface:"both"`, cli `["run","restore"]`, mcp `cw_run_restore`) + added to the payload-probe DEFERRED group (it mutates state + needs an archive fixture, so it is classified out of the deterministic bootstrap probe — an unclassified both-surface verb fails parity). CLI `case "restore":` in `handleRun` prints the result and FAIL-CLOSED sets `process.exitCode = 1` when `!result.ok` (mirrors the inspect-archive exit-1 pattern); `restore` added to the run-registry-subcommand guard Set + Usage string. MCP `capabilityTool("run.restore", …)` tool-def (archive/path/file/target/repo/cwd schema, mirroring `run.import`) + `case "cw_run_restore": return runRestoreArchive(runner, args);` in tool-call. Man-page `docs/run-registry-control-plane.7.md` gains a `run restore` block (fail-closed inspect -> import -> verify; refuses unverifiable bundles; `imported:null`/`verify:null` on refusal; exit 1 on `ok:false`), the CLI usage line, and `cw_run_restore` in the MCP list. | plugins/cool-workflow/src/capability-core.ts + plugins/cool-workflow/src/capability-registry.ts + plugins/cool-workflow/src/cli/handlers/run.ts + plugins/cool-workflow/src/mcp/tool-definitions.ts + plugins/cool-workflow/src/mcp/tool-call.ts + plugins/cool-workflow/docs/run-registry-control-plane.7.md + plugins/cool-workflow/test/run-restore-failclosed-smoke.js (new) + plugins/cool-workflow/docs/project-index.md (smoke count +1) + regenerated plugins/cool-workflow/dist/** + ITERATION_LOG.md | New `run-restore-failclosed-smoke` (TDD: failed before the verb existed — `run restore` hit `default:` and threw the Usage error, exit 1 at the happy case; passes after). Asserts (a) HAPPY: export a real run -> `run restore <archive> --target DIR --json` exit 0, JSON `ok:true` + `inspect.ok:true` + `verify.ok:true` + imported run id, and the run is present in the target's repo registry (`run list --cwd DIR --scope repo --json` -> `records[]` carries it); (b) FAIL-CLOSED on tamper: a digest-corrupted archive -> exit non-zero, output names the integrity refusal, `inspect.ok:false`, and the run is NOT left imported (no run dir on disk, registry does not list it) — no fabricated success; (c) `run import` STILL works unchanged (clean import exit 0, verifies). Each CLI spawn runs under an isolated `CW_HOME` + `TMPDIR` (hermetic). Regression: `run-import-tamper-failclosed-smoke` + `run-export-import-smoke` still green (import byte-identical). Project-index regenerated for the new smoke (149 smokes; index:check ok). | BUILD OK; check OK; parity:check OK (ok:true); index:check OK; onramp:check OK; run-restore-failclosed-smoke OK; run-import-tamper-failclosed-smoke OK; run-export-import-smoke OK | no (dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |
| 2 | Fix two audit-found defects in the cycle-1 verb. DEFECT 1 (double-verify): `runRestoreArchive` called `verifyImportedRun` a SECOND time after `importRun`, re-hashing every restored file and shipping the same verdict twice (under `imported.verification` AND `verify`). importRun ALREADY returns that verdict (`ImportResult.verification`) — so reuse it: `const verify = imported.verification;` (no second re-hash). The `ok: inspect.ok && verify.ok` carried a dead conjunct (`inspect.ok` is always true past the early-refuse return) — simplified to `ok: verify.ok` with a one-line comment. Result shape unchanged (`{ schemaVersion, ok, target, inspect, imported, verify, registry }`); CLI case unchanged (`if (!result.ok) process.exitCode = 1;`). DEFECT 2 (hollow test): the old tamper case corrupted file BYTES — which importRun's digest gate already throws on (empty stdout) — so the restore-specific assertions sat behind `if (r.stdout.trim())` and the test passed EVEN IF restore's fail-close were deleted. Rewrote it to exercise the REAL gap and assert UNCONDITIONALLY. The man-page `run restore` block + this batch's intro were corrected to the honest framing (import RUNS a verification and reports it but does not FAIL on it; restore reuses + fails-closed). No registry-summary or parity-matrix change (the `run.restore` summary was already accurate), so no parity-doc regen needed. | plugins/cool-workflow/src/capability-core.ts (reuse imported.verification; ok=verify.ok; doc comment) + plugins/cool-workflow/test/run-restore-failclosed-smoke.js (rewritten) + plugins/cool-workflow/docs/run-registry-control-plane.7.md (honest gap framing) + regenerated plugins/cool-workflow/dist/** + ITERATION_LOG.md | Rewrote `run-restore-failclosed-smoke` around a real DISCRIMINATOR. It hand-builds a run WITH real hash-chained content (`appendTelemetryAttestation` x2 + `recordTrustAuditEvent` x2, mirroring run-export-import-smoke) so the chains have something to break, then exports it. (A) HAPPY: `run restore --target DIR` exit 0; parse stdout JSON UNCONDITIONALLY, assert `ok===true` + `verify.ok===true`; run present in the target repo registry. (B) DISCRIMINATOR (the whole value): tamper ONE telemetry-ledger record's `attestation` so its recordHash no longer recomputes (`verifyTelemetryLedger` -> `telemetry-ledger-invalid`), then recompute that file's `sha256` + `sizeBytes` AND `integrity.manifestSha256` (digestManifest reproduced byte-for-byte and self-checked against the real archive) so `verifyArchiveFileDigests` still PASSES — an archive `run import` ACCEPTS but `verifyImportedRun` FAILS. On the SAME archive, asserted WITHOUT any `if(stdout)` gate: `run import` -> EXIT 0 + `verification.ok===false` (import fabricates), `run restore` -> EXIT NON-ZERO + `ok===false` + `verify.ok===false` + `inspect.ok===true` (restore fails-closed on what import only reports). (C) secondary byte-tamper -> restore exit≠0 inspect-first (`imported:null`, nothing on disk). MUTATION TEST (proof the test is real): deleted `if (!result.ok) process.exitCode = 1;` from the restore CLI case, rebuilt -> smoke went RED on the discriminator assertion (`restore EXITS NON-ZERO`, actual 0); restored the line, rebuilt -> GREEN. Regressions `run-import-tamper-failclosed-smoke` + `run-export-import-smoke` still green. | BUILD OK; check OK; parity:check OK (ok:true, mismatches:[]); index:check OK; readme:check OK; onramp:check OK; parity-doc-sync-smoke OK; run-restore-failclosed-smoke OK (+ mutation: RED without fail-close, GREEN with); run-import-tamper-failclosed-smoke OK; run-export-import-smoke OK | no (dev loop — review + PR, never tag) |

## Batch — make the github-release workflow idempotent (no false 422) (Unreleased)

> The `github-release.yml` workflow ran `gh release create` on every `v*` tag push, but the gated `release-flow.js --cut` ALSO creates the Release (with changelog notes) before it pushes the tag. So on cuts where local `gh` was authenticated, the Release already existed when CI ran, and `gh release create` failed with `HTTP 422: Release.tag_name already exists` — a false red run (seen on v0.1.93, v0.1.90, first v0.1.87). The release itself shipped fine each time; only the workflow went red. Fix: guard the create with a `gh release view` check — skip when the Release is already there, create only when it is not. Keeps the CI path as a safety net for cuts where local `gh` is missing (release-flow.js then skips, and CI makes the Release). Pure CI-infra change to a root workflow YAML, outside the plugin's onramp/parity gate scope; the real proof is the next tag push producing a green github-release run.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Make `github-release.yml` idempotent so a tag whose Release was already created by `release-flow.js --cut` no longer fails the workflow with HTTP 422. The `Create GitHub Release` step now runs `if gh release view "$ref" >/dev/null 2>&1` and echoes "already exists — nothing to do" instead of calling `gh release create` a second time; the create path (`--title --generate-notes --verify-tag`) is unchanged and still fires when no Release exists yet (cuts where local `gh` was unavailable). | .github/workflows/github-release.yml | None — pure CI workflow YAML at repo root, outside the plugin gate (onramp watches only `plugins/cool-workflow/`); there is no smoke harness for GitHub Actions YAML. Validated locally: `yaml.safe_load` parses the file, and `bash -n` accepts the new if/else run block. End-to-end proof is the next `v*` tag push showing a green github-release run (creates when absent, skips when present). | YAML OK (python yaml.safe_load); shell OK (bash -n); no plugin source touched so build/parity/onramp unaffected | no (CI-infra fix; nothing to release) |

## Batch — non-TTY agent progress opt-in (Unreleased)

> The live agent view + `[drive]` lines are TTY-gated (Rule of Silence). The man page
> (`agent-delegation-drive.7.md` "Live output") already promised `CW_AGENT_STREAM=1`
> opts a piped/CI run into a plain append-only trace, but the parent gate AND-ed on
> isTTY, so the opt-in was swallowed — code contradicting its own contract. The parent
> now honors `CW_AGENT_STREAM=1` even without a TTY; the env-unset default is

## Batch — close the last lock race on `state.json` itself (Unreleased)

> An architecture-review pass over state, persistence, and concurrency
> found that `saveCheckpoint` locks only the WRITE of `state.json`. Every
> verb that does load -> change -> save on its own (`cw dispatch`,
> `cw result`) left a window where two processes both load the same
> state and the later save silently drops the earlier change — the same
> lost-update class the v0.2.1 fix (#339) closed for `queue.json` and
> `triggers.json`, still open on the single source of truth itself. A
> new `run-state-lock-concurrency-smoke.js` reproduced it live: 6
> concurrent `cw result` calls on 6 tasks of the same run kept only 1
> completion before the fix.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Close the read-modify-write race on `state.json`: add `withRunStateLock(runId, cwd, fn)` in `run-store.ts`, which holds the `state.json` lock over the WHOLE load -> change -> save cycle instead of just the write; make `withFileLock` (`fs-atomic.ts`) re-entrant inside one process so nested `saveCheckpoint`/worker-failure-persist calls inside `fn` keep working unchanged; wire `dispatchRun` and `recordResultRun` (`pipeline-cli.ts`) through it. Also re-ran `sync:project-index` (`docs/project-index.md`) so the new smoke count stays in sync with the doc-drift guard. | `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/fs-atomic.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/run-store.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/pipeline-cli.ts`, matching `dist/**`, `plugins/cool-workflow/test/run-state-lock-concurrency-smoke.js` (new), `plugins/cool-workflow/docs/durable-state-and-locking.7.md`, `plugins/cool-workflow/docs/project-index.md` (regenerated) | New `run-state-lock-concurrency-smoke.js`: part A proves `withFileLock` is re-entrant in-process (nested same-target call runs under the held lock, single release); part B spawns one real child process per dispatched task of a live `architecture-review` run, holds them at a start line, and asserts all concurrent `cw result` recordings survive (failed on the pre-fix build: 1/6 kept). | BUILD OK; new smoke run 3x clean; `npm test` fast 34/34; conformance 101/101; `npm run test:gate` full 179-smoke sequential suite: first pass caught a stale `project-index.md` (unrelated doc-drift guard tripped by the new test file, fixed via `sync:project-index`), full suite green after | no (bugfix, no release) |

## Batch — post-v0.2.2 security hardening

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Four-agent security audit of v0.2.2 (process execution, filesystem, MCP surface, supply chain/CI) turned up two pipeline gate-bypass risks and two read-path traversal bugs; fix all four. (P2-1) `workflow_dispatch` on `npm-publish.yml` skipped the release-gate/verdict check entirely — a same-named branch with a matching `package.json` version and fresh `dist/` could publish with no gate ever having run; added a dispatch-only step requiring the ref to resolve to a real `refs/tags/v*` commit matching the checked-out HEAD, then re-running the same committed-verdict grep `release-gate.yml` runs on tag push. (P2-2) `npm install --no-package-lock` ran with lifecycle scripts enabled in the OIDC-privileged publish job and in `ci.yml`/`release-gate.yml`; added `--ignore-scripts` to all three (verified no locked package carries an install script). (P2-3) `cw man <topic>` (`man-cli.ts` `readManPage`)'s third candidate was a bare unsanitized `path.join(docsDir, topic)`, letting a traversal topic read any file on disk to stdout; added an `isContainedPath` check per candidate. (P2-4) `RunRegistry.locate`/`loadRun` (`run-registry-io.ts`) never called `assertSafeRunId` before joining `runId` into a filesystem path, unlike the sibling `run-store.ts` `loadRunFromCwd` — `run.show`/`resume`/`archive`/`rerun` (all routed through `locate()`) could probe or read any file named `state.json` via a crafted traversal id; added the same guard at both entry points. | `.github/workflows/npm-publish.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/release-gate.yml`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/man-cli.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/run-registry-io.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/test/man-run-registry-traversal-smoke.js` (new) | New `man-run-registry-traversal-smoke.js` passed standalone; full smoke suite 181/181 passed (incl. pre-existing `run-import-path-traversal-smoke.js`); `test:unit` 153/153; conformance 104/104 | BUILD OK; dist fresh; `release:check --skip-tests` clean after adding the smoke + this row | no (defensive hardening, no version bump) |
| 2 | Second pass on the same audit's P3 findings — seven more fixes. `scripts/agents/claude-p-agent.js` claimed "READ-ONLY by design" while granting `Bash` in `--allowedTools` (both the legacy and stream-json `claude -p` spawns); dropped it, kept `Read,Grep,Glob`, and strengthened `claude-p-agent-wrapper-smoke.js` to assert no Bash tool (it only checked for no Write before). `node-store.ts` `readNodeSnapshot`/`readNodeReplay` built the read path from a raw `snapshotId`/`replayId` with no separator filter, unlike every write-side path segment in the same file (`snapshotDir` already runs `nodeId` through `safeFileName`); now both read paths run the id through `safeFileName` too. `reclamation-io.ts` `freeBulk` did `fs.rmSync(path.join(runDir, entry.path), ...)` with no containment re-check (unlike `prepareFree`'s artifact-path checks) — a tampered `state.json` worker/artifact path could in principle escape `runDir`; added an `isContainedPath` guard before every delete. `fs-atomic.ts`'s three file-creation points (`writeJson`'s temp file, `durableAppendFileSync`'s append, the advisory lock's `wx` create) had no explicit mode, landing world-readable (`0644`) under a typical umask; pinned all three to `0o600` since this is run/ledger/registry state, not shared data. `github-release.yml` and two steps in `npm-publish.yml` spliced `${{ github.ref_name }}` / `${{ github.event.inputs.ref }}` / `${{ steps.tagref.outputs.ref }}` directly into `run:` script text — a crafted tag/ref containing shell metacharacters would be a command-injection primitive with the job's `GH_TOKEN`/OIDC token; routed all of them through `env:` + `"$VAR"` instead. `npm-publish.yml`'s `actions/checkout@v5`/`actions/setup-node@v5` were pinned to a moving major-version tag inside the OIDC-privileged publish job; pinned both to the commit SHA the tag currently resolves to, with a version comment. `block-unapproved-tag.sh`'s trigger regex missed the single most natural bypass form, `git push origin v0.2.3` (bare tag-shaped ref, no `--tags`/`refs/tags/` literal), and also missed `git tag --annotate` and a global flag before the subcommand (`git -C dir tag ...`); widened the regex to catch all three, verified against both new-match and no-false-positive cases (a `release/v0.2.2-verdict`-shaped branch push still stays allowed). | `plugins/cool-workflow/scripts/agents/claude-p-agent.js`, `plugins/cool-workflow/test/claude-p-agent-wrapper-smoke.js`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/node-store.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/reclamation-io.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/fs-atomic.ts`, `.github/workflows/github-release.yml`, `.github/workflows/npm-publish.yml`, `plugins/cool-workflow/scripts/block-unapproved-tag.sh`, `plugins/cool-workflow/test/block-unapproved-tag-smoke.js` | Full smoke suite 182/182 (incl. strengthened `claude-p-agent-wrapper-smoke.js` and `block-unapproved-tag-smoke.js` with 4 new cases); Added an `npm audit --audit-level=high` step to `ci.yml` right after install; local baseline is 0 vulnerabilities across the 3 devDependencies (typescript, @types/node, undici-types — zero runtime deps means that is the entire audited surface).
