## Batch — take out an array-copy-per-step time cost at 2 more spots (Unreleased)

> This batch's own review-follow-up (above) already found and fixed the
> `buildCompactGraphFromView` case of this same anti-pattern. A follow-up
> grep for the identical `map.set(key, [...(map.get(key) || []), id])`
> shape over the rest of `src/` turned up 2 more sites, fixed here the
> same way.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | Take out an O(N^2) time cost from two functions that made a new list copy on every step, in place of one push. `detectTies` (`core/multi-agent/candidate-scoring.ts`) and `derivePhases` (`core/state/migrations.ts`) both did `map.set(key, [...(map.get(key) \|\| []), id])` in a loop over every item — this makes a full copy of the group's list on every item put into it, so one big group costs O(N^2) time in place of O(N). Fixed by getting the list first, then using `.push()` if it is there, else making a new one-item list — output order and group make-up do not change (`.push()` keeps the same order the old spread-and-copy did). `derivePhases` is now made public (`export`) from its file so it can be tested on its own, apart from a different, older O(N^2) cost in `tasksForPhaseCompleted` (out of scope this cycle) that would else hide whether this one fix helped, when timed through the public `migrateRunState` path. | `plugins/cool-workflow/src/core/multi-agent/candidate-scoring.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/src/core/state/migrations.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/test/maruntime-candidate-tie-bucket-performance.test.js` (new), `plugins/cool-workflow/test/statecore-derive-phases-performance.test.js` (new), `ITERATION_LOG.md` | Before picking a time limit, the old (un-fixed) code was timed by hand: one cold call to `detectTies` over 20000 same-score items took 469ms, and one cold call to `derivePhases` over 20000 same-phase items took 664ms; after the fix, both take a few ms. New `maruntime-candidate-tie-bucket-performance.test.js` checks group make-up/order are not changed, then checks a 20000-item call stays under a 200ms time limit (the old code's 469ms would fail this). New `statecore-derive-phases-performance.test.js` checks the same for `derivePhases` with a 150ms time limit (the old code's 664ms would fail this). Both new tests were run by hand against the old code first and seen to fail, then against the fix and seen to pass. Full `test:unit` 158/158 (up from 156), full `test:gate` smoke run 191/191, black-box conformance suite 104/104 — all green. | BUILD OK; `npm run check` clean; `node scripts/purity-gate.js` clean; `node scripts/parity-check.js --check` clean (74 capabilities, 0 not-matching); conformance 104/104; `test:unit` 158/158; `test:gate` 191/191 | no (small fix cycle, dev loop — review + PR, no tag) |

## Batch — stop a smoke test from writing into the shared apps/ folder (Unreleased)

> Found by chance while at work on an unrelated change to run
> `test/run-all.js`'s smoke pool in known-run-time order. Not part of
> the performance-hardening batches above — a test-only fix, no `src/`
> or `dist/` file touched.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | A full run of the smoke set turned up a real, old bug: `execution-backends-smoke.js` calls `cw list` twice, over two different run paths, and checks that both give the SAME hash. In one full-set run, the two hashes did not match. Cause: `cw list` reads `apps/` to find its apps (`shell/workflow-app-loader.ts`). That `apps/` folder is shared by every smoke, but `run-all.js`'s `makeSandbox()` (which gives each smoke its own `CW_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME`/`HOME`/`TMPDIR`) never gave each smoke its own `apps/` root too. One smoke, `concurrent-subworkflow-cache-nesting-smoke.js`, put a throw-away test app right into the real, shared `apps/` folder, then took it away when done. A `cw list` run by a different smoke, at the same time, could see that throw-away app mid-write or mid-remove, giving a different list than another `cw list` call at another time — not a bug in the run-path code, a shared-folder race. Checked: this smoke passes alone 5 of 5 runs, and only fails when run with the full smoke set at once, which fits a shared-folder race, not a fixed bug in the run-path/backend code. Checked every other test file for the same shared-folder write; only this one file writes into the real `apps/` folder — a few other smokes (`budget-scaling-loop-smoke.js`, `loop-bounded-expansion-smoke.js`, `sigint-sigterm-drive-loop-smoke.js`, `sub-workflow-nesting-smoke.js`) already point at their own, private apps folder with the `CW_APPS_DIR` env value (read fresh at call time by `candidateAppsRoots()` in `shell/workflow-app-loader.ts`); this was the one smoke not yet moved to that same pattern. Fixed by moving the throw-away app under this smoke's own `work` tmp folder and setting `CW_APPS_DIR` to point there before the `drive()` call, cleared after — the same pattern the other smokes already use. No src/ or dist/ file was touched. | `plugins/cool-workflow/test/concurrent-subworkflow-cache-nesting-smoke.js` | Full unsampled `test:gate` (`node test/run-all.js`) run 3 times in a row: 191/191, 191/191, 191/191 — no fails, no flakes (the one earlier failure, on `one-way-boundary-smoke.js`, was a missing `node_modules` in this worktree, fixed with `npm install`, unrelated to this change). `concurrent-subworkflow-cache-nesting-smoke.js` and `execution-backends-smoke.js` also checked alone, both pass. | BUILD OK (test-only change, no src/dist touched); dist unchanged | no (test-infrastructure fix, no version bump) |

## Batch — make the onramp change-gate fail closed on a git error (Unreleased)

> Found by a 2026-07-12 security audit. A P2 bug in the onramp
> change-contract gate: a failed git call could be read as "no files
> changed," letting a real change pass the gate with no test and no
> log row.

| cycle | goal | files | tests | gate | tagged |
|-------|------|-------|-------|------|--------|
| 1 | The bug: `gitLines(cwd, args)` in `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/onramp.ts` gives back `[]` any time the `git` command it runs ends with a non-zero exit code — the same empty list it gives back when `git` runs fine and just finds no lines. `resolveChangedFiles()` calls `gitLines` twice to build the changed-file list, once for `git diff --name-only <baseRef> --` and once for `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`. If either of those `git` calls fails for any cause (a bad `baseRef`, a git error, a strange edge case), the changed-file list quietly becomes empty, with no way to see this happened. `evaluateOnrampContract([])` then finds no runtime/script/app files in that empty list, so it reports `ok:true` with zero issues — a real, unseen source change gets a free pass through the gate, even though the gate exists to make sure such a change has a test and an `ITERATION_LOG.md` row. Proved live: pointed `GIT_INDEX_FILE` at a broken file so `git rev-parse` (used to check the base ref) still works but the later `git diff` call fails with a non-zero exit code; before the fix, `onramp-check.js` printed `ok:true`, `changedFiles: []`, exit 0 — a false pass. The fix: added one new function, `gitLinesOrThrow(cwd, args)`, right after `gitLines`. It runs the same way, but when the `git` call fails (bad exit code, or fails to start) it throws an `Error` with a clear message, in place of giving back `[]`. `resolveChangedFiles()`'s two `gitLines(...)` calls are now `gitLinesOrThrow(...)` calls; nothing else in that function changed. `gitLines` and `gitOne` are left exactly as they were and keep being used by `resolveBaseRef()` (an optional merge-base check) and `gitRoot()`, which want a soft "try, and fall back" result, not a throw. Why this is safe: `scripts/onramp-check.js`'s `main()` already wraps its call in a top-level try/catch that sets `process.exitCode = 1` and prints the error, so a thrown `Error` now makes the check fail with a clear message in place of a silent, wrong pass. `src/cli/entry.ts`'s top-level catch does the same for `cw doctor --onramp`, turning any thrown error into a clean `cw: <message>` on stderr with exit code 1. Checked the whole `src/` tree: no file other than `onramp.ts`'s own `buildDoctorOnramp` and `scripts/onramp-check.js` calls `resolveChangedFiles`, so no caller wrongly assumes it can never throw. Only the one code path that must fail closed was changed; every other git call keeps its old, soft, "try and fall back" behavior — the smallest fix that closes the real hole. | `plugins/cool-workflow/src/shell/onramp.ts`, `plugins/cool-workflow/dist/shell/onramp.js`, `plugins/cool-workflow/test/onramp-check-smoke.js` | Extended `onramp-check-smoke.js` with a new case: point `GIT_INDEX_FILE` at a broken file (so `HEAD` still resolves fine but the later `git diff --name-only HEAD --` call fails), run `scripts/onramp-check.js --changed-from HEAD --check`, and check it now throws (non-zero exit) with a message that says the git command could not be resolved. Ran this new case by hand against the pre-fix code first: it failed with `AssertionError: onramp-check must fail closed...` (pre-fix code printed `ok:true`, exit 0) — proving the case would have caught the real bug. Ran again against the fix: passes. Full `onramp-check-smoke.js` run alone: pass. Full `npm run test:fast` (204 smokes, `--concurrency auto`): 203/204 pass; the 1 failure (`verify-bump-reproduction-smoke.js`, a slow release-flow test unrelated to `onramp.ts`) was a timeout under load, not a real fail — re-run alone, it passes clean. Full `npm run test:unit`: 161/161 pass. | `npm run build --prefix plugins/cool-workflow` clean; `npm run check --prefix plugins/cool-workflow` (tsc, no emit) clean; `node plugins/cool-workflow/test/onramp-check-smoke.js` pass | no (small fix cycle, dev loop -- review + PR, no tag) |
